# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_27776807.pdf
 
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTER 8.68, CAMPING ON PUBLIC 
PROPERTY, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, EXPANDING THE GENERAL PROHIBITION 
ON CAMPING TO ALL PUBLIC PROPERTY OF THE CITY, CREATING 
A PERMITTING PROCESS, MAKING CONFORMING AMENDMENTS 
AND REPEALING OBSOLETE PROVISIONS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 8.68.025, Sleeping on Sidewalks, Streets, Alleys or Within 
Doorways Prohibited, and Section 8.68.035, Camping Allowed with Lawful Permit; Permit 
Administration, of Chapter 8.68, Camping on Public Property, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby created to read: 
 
8.68.025 - Sleeping on sidewalks, streets, alleys or within doorways prohibited. 
 
A. No person shall sleep on any public sidewalk, street or alleyway at any time as a matter of 
individual and public safety. 
 
B. No person may sleep in any pedestrian or vehicular entrance to public or private property 
abutting a public sidewalk. 
 
C. Any person who violates this section is guilty of a misdemeanor punishable by a fine of 
fifty dollars ($50.00). Any person found in violation of this section may also be 
immediately removed from the premises. Upon conviction for a violation of this section, if 
a defendant demonstrates that after receiving the citation and before the hearing, they 
meaningfully engaged with public or private resources or service providers to address the 
reasons that led them to be in violation, the court shall consider that information when 
determining the appropriate sentence. 
 
8.68.035 - Camping allowed with lawful permit; permit administration. 
 
A. Notwithstanding the prohibitions imposed under Section 8.68.030 of this chapter, a person 
may camp on public property of the city upon the issuance of a camping permit by the city 
clerk and payment of the associated permit fee as provided by this section. All applications 
for a camping permit shall be submitted to the city clerk on forms provided by the clerk 
and shall be submitted not less than ten (10) business days before the date the camping will 
occur. No permit may be issued under this section except upon the approval of the clerk 
and the director of the community recreation and events department. 
 
B. Before the issuance of any camping permit under this section, the director of the 
community recreation and events department shall: 
 
1:24 pm, Nov 20, 2024

 
Page 2 of 7 
 
1. Determine and keep a record of all parcels of public property of the city that are 
appropriate for camping and for which a camping permit may be issued under this 
section. In identifying any parcel for camping under this paragraph, the director shall 
only select those parcels, and the locations in each parcel, that will not overburden the 
surrounding community in terms of noise, traffic and environmental impact; 
 
2. Prescribe the attendant camping permit fee for camping on a parcel of public property, 
which fee may vary by parcel of public property, but which shall only be in an amount 
sufficient to defer the costs and impacts associated with the camping activity; and 
 
3. Prescribe permit conditions, the intent of which shall be provide for the proper 
maintenance and administration of the parcels upon which camping occurs and to 
protect public health and safety of the camping participants and the community. 
 
C. Any person camping on a parcel of public property without a permit as provided by this 
section shall be guilty of a misdemeanor punishable by a fine of not more than fifty dollars 
($50.00). Each day a person camps without a permit shall constitute a separate a distinct 
violation of this section. 
 
Section 2.  That Chapter 8.68, Camping on Public Property, of Title 8, Health and Safety, 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended to read: 
 
8.68.010 - Legislative findings and intent.  
 
A. It is found and declared that: 
 
1. The act of camping on public property tends to endanger the health and safety of those 
engaged in such camping as well as the public at large; 
 
2. Campsites positioned on public property of the city of Cheyenne, including on 
undeveloped parcels, public rights-of-way, under bridges and on or near sidewalks, also 
undermine the public's ability to use that property for its intended purpose; 
 
3. Camping on public property also creates unsafe and unsanitary living conditions which 
pose a threat to the peace, health and safety of the people who are camping and the 
community; 
 
4. Crow Creek serves as a vital component of the drainage network of the city of 
Cheyenne, offers valuable habitat for local flora and fauna and the creek's health 
requires special attention; 
 
5. 4. Some individuals have come to increasingly rely on public property adjacent to 
Crow Creek for the purpose of camping and the establishment of campsites creates 
significant hazards for Cheyenne, including damaging water quality through the 
introduction of trash, human waste and other materials; 
 

 
Page 3 of 7 
 
6. 5. Currently portions of Crow Creek are severely Many areas in Cheyenne are 
materially impacted through by camping on public property, demonstrated by the 
presence of trash, discarded items of personal property, discarded shelter elements, and 
human waste; 
 
7. The water quality of Crow Creek is of particular concern with significantly elevated 
levels of E. coli, a sub-group of the fecal coliform group that can cause extraintestinal 
illness and lead to diseases including typhoid fever and cholera; 
 
8. 6. Additionally, continued trash deposits into Crow Creek the waterways of the city 
negatively impact the city of Cheyenne's ability to effectively manage surface water 
runoff because trash can obstruct discharge pipes and otherwise impede the flow of 
surface water through the city's surface water runoff infrastructure; 
 
9. 7. Moreover, camping on public property along Crow Creek not only presents dangers 
to the health and safety of those people camping but poses a threat to persons and 
property, including residents and property owners near those areas; 
 
10. 8. During high and extreme fire conditions, public property along Crow Creek poses 
a can face unique fire danger dangers due to dry brush and other abundant fuel sources, 
and combined with potential unregulated campfires, stoves and other incendiary 
camping supplies, such danger is only increased; 
 
11. It is difficult for emergency personnel to evacuate people camping on public property 
along Crow Creek during a fire event, which places additional strains on the city of 
Cheyenne's emergency response programs and personnel; 
 
12. This chapter's regulations are intended strictly to regulate the use of publicly owned 
property along Crow Creek, and are not intended to regulate activities on private 
property; and 
 
13. 9. The enactment of this chapter is necessary to protect the peace, health, and safety 
of the city and its inhabitants. 
 
8.68.020 - Applicability; definitions. 
 
 
A. This chapter shall not be construed to modify, limit or supersede any requirement imposed 
under the Unified Development Code (UDC). To the extent there is a conflict between this 
chapter and the UDC, the UDC shall govern. 
 
B. As used in this chapter: 
 
1. "Abate" or "abatement" means the removal of all personal property, garbage and other 
foreign materials together with the reclamation and restoration of a campsite to its 
natural or lawful purpose; 
 

 
Page 4 of 7 
 
2. "Campsite" or "camping" means an area where a person temporarily resides or dwells 
through use of a tent, tarpaulin, lean-to, sleeping bag, bedroll, blanket or any other form 
of cover or protection from the elements other than clothing; 
 
3. "City" means as defined by Section 1.04.020 of the city code; 
 
4. "Department" means the compliance department of the city of Cheyenne; 
 
5. "Greater Cheyenne greenway" means the ten-foot wide, reinforced concrete path 
established by the city of Cheyenne that can be utilized by pedestrians and bicyclists 
throughout the city and surrounding areas; 
 
6. 5. "Personal property" means any item that readily appears to belong to a person and 
has apparent value or utility in its present condition. Personal property does not include 
building materials, metal, makeshift shelters, rigid plastic, garbage, contraband, debris 
or waste. 
 
7. 6. "Public property" means any street, alley, sidewalk, bike path, greenway, park, 
parkway or any other structure or area encompassed within the public right-of-way and 
includes any other grounds, buildings or other facilities owned, leased or managed by 
the city, regardless of whether the public property is vacant or occupied and actively 
used for any public purpose; 
 
8. 7. "Reside or dwell" includes, without limitation, conducting such activities as eating, 
sleeping or the storage of personal possessions; and 
 
9. 8. "Unlawful campsite" means any area of public property as specified by Section 
8.68.030(A) of this chapter upon which any person camps engages in the act of 
camping. 
 
8.68.030 - Camping and unlawful campsites on public property prohibited; public property 
occupancy restriction; exception; penalty.  
 
A. It is unlawful for any campsite to be situated on public property adjacent to or contiguous 
to Crow Creek located within the incorporated limits of the city. of Cheyenne between 
Interstate 25 and Morrie Avenue. All Every unlawful campsites campsite shall be subject 
to abatement by the department in accordance with Section 8.68.040 of this chapter. 
 
B. Between the hours of 12:00 a.m. and 5:00 a.m., No person shall use or otherwise occupy 
any public property adjacent to or contiguous to Crow Creek located within the 
incorporated limits of the city of Cheyenne between Interstate 25 and Morrie Avenue, 
except as authorized under subsection (C) of this section. for camping purposes unless the 
person holds a valid permit issued in accordance with Section 8.68.035 of this chapter. 
 

 
Page 5 of 7 
 
C. The prohibitions imposed under subsection (B) of this section shall not apply to any person 
actively using the greater Cheyenne greenway located within the subject area as a means 
to commute or otherwise travel for business or for recreational purposes. 
 
D. C. 
Any person who violates subsection (B) of this section is guilty of a misdemeanor 
punishable by imprisonment for not more than six (6) months, a fine of not more than seven 
hundred fifty dollars ($750.00), or both ($50.00). Any person found in violation of 
subsection (B) of this section may also be immediately removed from the premises. Upon 
conviction for a violation of subsection (B) of this section, if a defendant demonstrates that 
after receiving the citation and before the hearing, they meaningfully engaged with public 
or private resources or service providers to address the reasons that led them to be in 
violation, the court shall consider that information when determining the appropriate 
sentence. 
 
8.68.040 - Abatement of unlawful campsites; notice requirement; exception. 
 
A. Any unlawful campsite identified by a law enforcement officer or other Cheyenne city 
employee is subject to abatement by the department. Upon the identification of an unlawful 
campsite the law enforcement officer or Cheyenne city employee shall promptly notify the 
department of the campsite and its location. 
 
B. Upon receiving notice of an unlawful campsite under subsection (A) of this section, the 
department shall assess the condition of the campsite, prioritizing abatement on campsites 
that pose the greatest threat to public health and safety. When the department abates any 
campsite in accordance with this section, the public works departments department shall 
coordinate with the department to provide abatement assistance and support when 
necessary. 
 
C. Except for emergency abatements as authorized under subsection (E) of this section, the 
department may only abate an unlawful campsite after satisfying the following conditions: 
 
1. Providing all persons present at the unlawful campsite verbal or written notice of the 
date and time of the scheduled abatement; 
 
2. Posting notice around the area of the unlawful campsite at intervals sufficient to advise 
any person entering the campsite of the date and time of the scheduled abatement. The 
department shall not be required to repost any previously posted notice that has been 
removed or destroyed prior to the scheduled abatement; and 
 
3. The expiration of a forty-eight- (48) hour notice period following the delivery of the 
notice as required under paragraphs (1) and (2) of this subsection. 
 
D. The notices required under subsection (C) of this section shall include a statement: 
 
1. Advising that both camping on and locating campsites in the area are on public property 
of the city is prohibited under this chapter and that all any person violating this 

 
Page 6 of 7 
 
prohibition is subject to criminal prosecution and that any unlawful campsites are 
campsite is subject to abatement by the city; and 
 
2. That all personal property and any other property, contraband, litter or waste remaining 
at the an unlawful campsite after the expiration of the forty-eight- (48) hour notice 
period shall be considered abandoned and subject to removal and disposal by the city. 
 
E. The required tolling of the forty-eight- (48) hour notice period before an unlawful campsite 
may be abated as required by paragraph (C)(3) of this section shall not apply and the 
campsite may be immediately abated by the department when: 
 
a. 1. There are grounds for law enforcement officials to believe that illegal activities are 
occurring at an established camping site; 
 
b. 2. The chief of the fire department, or his or her designee, determines abatement is 
necessary due to an emergency that presents an imminent threat to real property or to 
the health and safety of any person; or 
 
c. 3. An unlawful campsite is in a watercourse and there is an imminent threat of 
flooding due to a flash flood warning, flood warning or flood watch issued by the 
National Weather Service and confirmed by the city engineer. 
 
F. Any personal property collected by the city because of an emergency abatement as 
authorized under subsection (E) of this section, which property does not constitute evidence 
of a crime, shall be retained by the city for not less than forty-eight (48) hours during which 
time it shall be made reasonably available for and released to the person confirming 
ownership. 
 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_26743524.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
City of Cheyenne Governing Body 
FROM: 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
November 1, 2024  
SUBJECT: 
Annual Write-Off Per Resolution 4970 
On November 12, 2024, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance 
Committee to write off $72,176.69 in uncollectible accounts of the City and $8,329.40 for 
the Board of Public Utilities sanitation billings.  Additionally, $46,916.00 in unpaid parking 
tickets from 2020 are included.  Per Resolution 4970, debts owed to the City that are at least four 
years old, the customer is deceased or their debt has been discharged in bankruptcy 
are considered “uncollectible”.   
The type of debt under consideration for write off is as follows: 
Please note that approval of this resolution does not prevent further collection efforts on the 
accounts listed.  The collection agency will keep working on these debts until the statute of 
limitations ends, even if the accounts are removed from our financial records.   
As always, if you have any questions on the policies and procedures in place, please give me a call 
at 637-6336 or email at rlockman@cheyennecity.org.

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE CITY TREASURER TO 
DISCHARGE UNCOLLECTIBLE DEBTS PURSUANT TO WYOMING 
STATUTE § 16-4-502." 
 
WHEREAS, the City Treasurer and Board of Public Utilities Administration Manager are 
responsible for collecting debts owed to the City of Cheyenne using a process that includes internal 
efforts, legal remedies and the use of collection agencies; 
 
WHEREAS, once accounts reach a certain age without successful collection, generally 
accepted accounting principles (GAAP) require that accounts be written off the financial 
accounting records once management determines the receivable to be uncollectible after diligent 
and reasonable efforts were made to collect; 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution # 4970, the Governing Body deems debts owed to the 
City to be uncollectible, that are four or more years old, the debtor is deceased, or the debt has 
been discharged in bankruptcy proceedings;  
 
WHEREAS, collection efforts such as liens filed by the City and accounts being sent to the 
City’s collection agency have failed;  
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 16-4-502 provides for discharge of uncollectible debts 
upon proper certification to the Governing Body;  
 
WHEREAS, this certification includes the name of the debtor, a description of the goods 
or services for which the debt was incurred, the amount of the debt, the date when the debt became 
due and payable, and an explanation of what actions have been taken to collect the debt; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City Treasurer has prepared the required report of uncollectible debts to 
be written off, a copy of which is attached to and incorporated as part of this Resolution. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING that the City Treasurer is authorized to discharge the City of 
Cheyenne’s uncollectible debts including parking tickets in the amount of one hundred and 
nineteen thousand ninety two dollars and sixty nine cents ($119,092.69) and the Board of Public 
Utilities sanitation debts in the amount of eight thousand three hundred and twenty nine dollars 
and forty cents ($8,329.40). 
 
 
 
 
7:14 pm, Nov 04 2024

PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White, Planner II 
Subject: PUDC-24-199 / Holliday Park Project - Future Land Use Map Amendment 
Date: 
November 12, 2024 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Future Land Use Map Amendment. This is approved by Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
11/12/2024 
• 
Public Services Committee  
11/19/2024 
• 
Final Consideration 
 
11/25/2024 
 
Related Items to be introduced on 11/12/2024: 
• 
PUDC-24-195: Zone Change 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their November 4, 2024 meeting and unanimously 
recommended the Governing Body approve this application. 
 
Board of County Commissioners Recommendation:  
This item will be heard by the Board of County Commissioners on November 19, 2024 for comment only. 
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed.

1. Resolution 
2. Planning Commission Staff Report 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION CERTIFYING PLANNING COMMISSION ACTION 
AMENDING THE FUTURE LAND USE MAPS OF PLANCHEYENNE 
CHANGING THE LAND USE CATEGORY FROM OPEN SPACE AND 
PARKS TO MIXED-USE COMMERCIAL FOR PROPERTY GENERALLY 
LOCATED NORTHEAST OF THE INTERSECTION OF MORRIE 
AVENUE AND EAST LINCOLNWAY." 
 
 
WHEREAS, PlanCheyenne, as amended by PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress 
adopted by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne by Resolution No. 5573, is the Master 
Plan for the City of Cheyenne and the Comprehensive Plan for portions of Laramie County adopted 
by both the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County Board of County 
Commissioners; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the intent of the Future Land Use plan is not to predetermine specific land 
uses for individual parcels or specific locations for uses or facilities, but to provide a general 
framework for neighborhoods and activity centers organized around roadway networks and major 
parks and open space; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Brandon Swain, a property owner within Cheyenne, has initiated an 
amendment to the Future Land Use map to amend the land use category from Open Space and 
Parks to Mixed-use Business for land to be known as Lot 1, Block 1, Holliday Park; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendments approved by the Governing Body pursuant to the criteria 
set forth on Page 18 of PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress, as amended; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Laramie County Board of County Commissioners reviewed the proposed 
future land use map amendment and were given the opportunity to make comments; 
 
 
WHEREAS, a public hearing to receive public input and comments on the proposed Future 
Land Use map amendment for the above-described property was held before the City of Cheyenne 
Planning Commission on November 4, 2024, and the Commission approved the proposed Future 
Land Use map amendment; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendment processes and procedures required by PlanCheyenne, as 
amended, have been followed with regard to the request by Brandon Swain to amend the Future 
Land Use map category for the above-described property. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne Governing Body hereby certifies 
Planning Commission action amending the Land Use Plan for PlanCheyenne changing the land 
use category from Open Space and Parks to Mixed-use Commercial, as shown in Exhibit A, in 
accordance with the procedures outlined in PlanCheyenne and Wyoming Statutes § 15-1-503 and 
§ 15-1-505; 
 
6:49 pm, Nov 04 2024

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that staff is authorized to amend affected maps of 
PlanCheyenne to reflect the aforementioned changes; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this resolution shall become effective after its 
passage, approval and publication.  
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

PUDC-24-199 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
NOVEMBER 4, 2024 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-24-199:  Future Land Use Plan Amendment from Open Space and 
Parks to Mixed-use Commercial 
REQUEST: 
A request to amend the Future Land Use Plan from Open Space and Parks 
to Mixed-use Commercial. 
LOCATION: 
Northeast of the intersection of Morrie Avenue and E Lincolnway. 
APPLICANT: 
Brandon Swain 
OWNER: 
Brandon Swain and the City of Cheyenne 
ZONING: 
MUR Mixed-use Residential and P Public, proposed rezoning to MUB 
Mixed-use Business.  
PURPOSE: 
To match a requested zone change of a portion of the area to MUB for 
development of the property. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-199 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Resolution amending the Future Land Use Plan for a portion of land northeast of 
the intersection of Morrie Avenue and E Lincolnway as shown in the Future Land Use Map provided 
from Open Space and Parks to Mixed-use Commercial.  
    
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
PlanCheyenne (2016 Community Plan) 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting amending the Future Land Use Map for roughly 0.75 acres of land from Open 
Space and Parks to Mixed-use Commercial to accommodate a zone change to permit a mixed-use 
development. The property is partially under private ownership and partially under a surplus sale agreement. 
As part of the surplus sale agreement, the new owner was required to amend the future land use map. The 
property was not developed for park purposes. 
 
A concurrent application for a zoning map amendment has been submitted. The zoning map amendment will 
accompany the future land use amendment at City Council. If the application for the zoning map amendment 
is approved, the applicant will be required to submit a site plan application for the new development. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is shown as Open Space and Parks in the Future Land Use Plan. The Parks and Open Space 
category includes existing and planned future parks and open space areas.

PUDC-24-199 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve an amendment to the Future Land Use Map of the 
Comprehensive Plan, the Commission must make the following considerations outlined in the 2014 
Community Plan (Build Chapter page 3-3).  A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) BENEFICIAL OR ADDRESSES ISSUES - The proposed amendment is beneficial to the community 
or addresses issues not foreseen during the planning process.    
 
The proposed amendment to Mixed-use Commercial would be beneficial at the proposed 
location as it is a major intersection. The proposed area is also going to be under private 
ownership and should no longer be considered Open Space and Parks. The proposed 
development is mixed use with commercial on the first floor and residential above which fits the 
designation of Mixed-use Commercial.   
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 1. 
 
2) COMPATIBLY - The proposed amendment is compatible with the surrounding area, and the 
goals and policies of the Plan.  
 
The subject property is adjacent to Community Business and Urban Residential to the west and 
Mixed-use Commercial to the south. The proposed Mixed-use Commercial designation would be 
compatible with the surrounding area as it would allow for a mix of both commercial and 
residential which both are present in this area.  
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 2. 
   
3) IMPACT MITIGATION - The proposed amendment will address and mitigate impacts on 
transportation, services, and facilities.  
 
Transportation: 
The transportation network in this area of the City is currently developed. All impacts to the 
transportation network will be addressed through the platting and site plan processes.  
 
Services: 
The proposed change will not have any adverse effects on the provision of services to the 
subject property. Service providers, such as the fire department, will be included in platting and 
site planning processes to assess service needs. 
 
Facilities: 
The proposed change will likely not require new facilities outside of the development area. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 3. 
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-199 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
4) SERVICE PROVISION - The proposed amendment will have minimal effect on service provision, 
including adequacy or availability of urban facilities and services, and is compatible with 
existing and planned service provision  
 
This land is within City limits and urban facilities and services are available to the subject 
properties. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 4. 
 
5) ANNEXATION - The proposed amendment does not jeopardize the City’s ability to annex the 
property. 
 
The subject property is already within City limits.   
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 5. 
 .   
6) URBAN SERVICES AREA - The proposed amendment is consistent with the logical expansion of 
the Urban Services Area. 
 
This land is currently within the Urban Services Area and does not expand the current area. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 6. 
 
7) STRICT ADHERENCE - Strict adherence to the Plan would result in a situation neither intended 
nor in keeping with other key elements and policies of the Plan. 
 
Strict adherence to the Plan may result in unintended consequences. There is already a single-
family home at this location and the rest of the proposed area is currently a parking lot. The 
current designation would not allow for the redevelopment of this corner with a mixed-use 
building, which will include residential units.  
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 7. 
 
8) PUBLIC WELFARE AND CONSISTENCY – The proposed plan amendment will promote the public 
welfare and will be consistent with the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne and the elements 
thereof. 
 
Staff is satisfied that the proposed application will not have any adverse effects on public 
welfare. Furthermore, the application accomplishes the following policies contained within 
PlanCheyenne: 
 
• 
Policy 1.1.A: Balanced Mix of Land Uses 
The Future Land Use Plan establishes a balanced mix of residential and non-residential 
land uses throughout the Cheyenne Area. The City and County should use the Future 
Land Use Plan and this Plan’s policies as the main tools for guiding decisions about 
growth and development. 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-199 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
• 
Policy 1.1.B. Flexibility to Market Demands: This goal notes that the Future Land Use 
Plan does not predetermine all uses on individual parcels and is meant to provide 
flexibility. The future land use map amendment to MU-C will allow for a mix of both 
commercial and residential in this area of the community.  
 
• 
Policy 1.4.A. Infill Opportunities: The future land use map amendment may facilitate 
infill opportunities for residential expansion. The community is currently facing a 
housing shortage and allowing residential in more places and providing more 
opportunities for residential expansion will aid in addressing the shortage. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
No other plans are applicable for this area.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff recommends a Mixed-use Commercial Land Use Category for this property because it is 
located at the intersection of two major streets and allows for redevelopment with a mixed-use 
building. A future land use amendment to Mixed-use Commercial will support future rezoning 
initiatives in the area that permit the development of the land with new developments that will 
support the adjacent neighborhoods and commercial areas. The area is not appropriate to be 
designated Open Space and Parks as it was not developed for park purposes and is being 
removed from the ownership of the owner of the rest of the nearby park. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan as submitted by the applicant and 
expanded upon by staff (Staff Recommendation). 
2. Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan subject to modifications. 
3. Deny the Resolution to not amend the Land Use Plan.  The Planning Commission must conclude 
that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings for denial 
must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Resolution until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite specific 
reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan for a portion of land 
northeast of the intersection of Morrie Avenue and E Lincolnway as shown in the applicants submitted 
Future Land Use map from Open Space and Parks to Mixed-use Commercial.      
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-199 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map  
3. Application 
4. Applicants Future Land Use Map – Area to be amended.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_79962019.pdf
RESOLUTION NO.   
 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION DIRECTING CITY STAFF TO EVALUATE AND 
DETERMINE 
AN 
EFFECTIVE 
DRAINAGE 
PLAN 
FOR 
THE 
INTERSECTION OF BEVANS STREET AND WILLS ROAD AND THE 
ADJACENT AREAS." 
 
WHEREAS, community members from the area surrounding the intersection of Bevans 
Street and Wills Road have reported concerns on this intersection multiple times during warm 
temperatures and cold temperatures, to include events of massive flooding and the buildup of 
sheets of ice; 
 
WHEREAS, the surface water issues associated with this intersection have resulted in 
many issues including stalled vehicles stranded in the flooded intersection and traffic detours 
through the adjacent neighborhoods due to vehicle crashes and other emergency events on nearby 
Ridge Road; 
 
WHEREAS, community members in this area are also fearful of flood waters approaching 
their homes during weather events and of the potential loss of life and property damage in the 
event of vehicle collisions during the winter months when the intersection is covered in ice; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne recognizes the need to remedy 
the collection of surface water during weather events at the intersection of Bevans Street and Wills 
Road and provide for adequate drainage; 
 
WHEREAS, in recognition of drainage issue surrounding the intersection of Bevans Street 
and Wills Road, on November 8, 2023, the City of Cheyenne executed a professional services 
agreement with Ardurra Group, Inc. for a drainage improvement feasibility investigation; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body realizes that a project of this magnitude likely requires 
many upstream and downstream components to alleviate the surface water issues associated with 
this area; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body recognizes that property acquisitions and negotiations 
with other governmental agencies may also be necessary to alleviate flooding in this area;  
 
WHEREAS, funding mechanisms to address the surface water management concerns at 
the intersection of Bevans Street and Wills Road are best identified by the City Engineer's Office, 
and funding for such a project may include funds from General Fund reserves, Ward contingency 
funds and other funding available to be approved by the Governing Body. 
 
3:40 pm, Nov 05, 2024

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Office of the City Engineer, in coordination with other 
City Staff, is hereby requested to update the Governing Body on the status of the previously 
executed professional services agreement with Ardurra, and to otherwise evaluate and determine 
an effective drainage plan for the intersection of Bevans Street and Wills Road and the adjacent 
areas. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_17365933.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION BY THE GOVERNING BODY DESIGNATING A PAID 
ON-STREET PARKING DISTRICT FOR DOWNTOWN CHEYENNE IN 
ACCORDANCE WITH SECTION 10.60.015 OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING." 
 
WHEREAS, on July 22, 2024, the Governing Body adopted Resolution No. 4582, which created 
Section 10.60.015 of the city code and established the Paid On-Street Parking Districts Program for the 
City of Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with Section 10.60.015, the Governing Body is responsible for 
designating for any paid on-street parking district in the city; 
 
WHEREAS, upon designation of any paid on-street parking district, the Parking Division within 
the Cheyenne Police Department must establish appropriate on-street parking zones and reasonable on-
street parking rates with the district; and 
 
WHEREAS, as required by the Section 10.60.015 of the city code, the Governing Body has 
determined that a paid on-street parking district should be created for the downtown area of Cheyenne 
as provided herein. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that in accordance with Section 10.60.015 of the city code, the geographic 
area for the downtown paid on-street parking district is hereby designated as provided by the map 
appended to this Resolution, which map is incorporated herein by reference; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the downtown paid on-street parking district designated 
herein shall include both sides of the roadways forming the boundaries of the district; and 
 
BE IT FINALLY RESOLVED, that the designation of the downtown paid on-street parking 
district shall not become effective until the on-street parking zones and on-street parking rates have been 
established by the Parking Division within the Cheyenne Police Department in accordance with Section 
10.60.015 of the city code. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_71165899.pdf
 
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
City of Cheyenne Governing Body 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
November 20, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
One-Time Payment Resolution 
 
 
On November 25, 2024, the Governing Body will be referring to the Finance Committee the 
attached resolution authorizing a one-time payment for all permanent full-time and part-time 
employees of the City of Cheyenne. This resolution proposes a $1,000 payment for permanent 
part-time employees and a $2,000 payment for permanent full-time employees.  The goal of this 
resolution is to recognize the outstanding contribution of City employees, particularly in achieving 
significant cost savings during fiscal year 2024. 
The estimated cost of this one-time payment is as follows: 
• $1,026,000 for the General Fund.  This comprises 440 full-time and 77 part-time 
employees and includes all employees paid for by the General Fund, as well as specific 
exceptions including the Youth Alternatives Grant Fund, Juvenile Justice Fund and the 
Law Enforcement Grants Fund.  These funds cannot sustain the cost of this one-time 
payment or are not permissible by grant requirements. 
• $428,000 for all other Funds.  This covers 151 full-time and 89 part-time employees of 
various other funds, including Solid Waste, Transportation Planning, Transit, Weed and 
Pest, Recreation Programs, Federal Grants, One Percent, Specific Purpose Option Tax, 
Fleet Maintenance, Civic Center and Ice and Events Center.  
Please note that these are estimates as we won’t know the final number of employees who qualify 
until after November 30, 2024.   
If you have questions, feel free to me a call at 637-6336 or email at rlockman@cheyennecity.org.  
 

Page 1 of 2 
 
RESOLUTION NO. ___________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING A ONE-TIME PAYMENT FROM 
FISCAL YEAR 2024 BUDGET SAVINGS GIVEN TO FULL-TIME AND 
PERMANENT PART-TIME EMPLOYEES OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
(EXCLUDING ELECTED OFFICIALS AND EMPLOYEES OF THE 
CHEYENNE BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES)." 
 
 
WHEREAS, the success and financial health of the City of Cheyenne depends on the 
dedication and innovative efforts of City employees; 
 
WHEREAS, during the fiscal year ending June 30, 2024, City employees have 
demonstrated exceptional commitment to reducing costs and improving operating efficiency;  
 
WHEREAS, these cost-saving measures have resulted in a significant positive impact on 
the City’s General Fund budget, achieving savings of Three Million, Two Hundred Twelve 
Thousand, Eight Hundred Seventy Dollars ($3,212,870) in Fiscal Year 2024; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne’s Governing Body recognizes the importance of 
acknowledging and rewarding City staff for these cost savings, and wishes to provide a one-time 
payment of two-thousand dollars ($2,000) to each full-time employee and one-thousand dollars 
($1,000) to each permanent part-time employee excluding elected officials and those employed by 
the separate Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that all full-time employees, defined as working thirty (30) or 
more hours per week, who are employed as of November 30, 2024, will receive a two thousand 
dollar ($2,000) one-time payment in December 2024, subject to federal withholding requirements. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that permanent part-time employees will receive a one 
thousand dollar ($1,000) one-time payment in December 2024, subject to federal withholding 
requirements, if they meet the following guidelines: 
1. The employee works less than thirty (30) hours per week and has received a paycheck 
each month from November 2023 to November 2024; or 
 
2. If an employee was hired after December 1, 2023, they must have received a paycheck 
each month from their hire date to November 2024; and 
 
3. The employee must be employed by the City of Cheyenne on November 30, 2024; and 
 
4. The employee must not be a seasonal employee. 
3:03 pm, Nov 20 2024

Page 2 of 2 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_51651289.pdf
RESOLUTION NO.   
 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION ENCOURAGING CITY STAFF TO SOLICIT PUBLIC 
COMMENT 
ON 
CHANGES 
TO 
THE 
ADMINISTRATION 
OF 
SANITATION PICKUP LOCATIONS AND FURTHER DIRECTING CITY 
STAFF TO COORDINATE WITH UNIVERUS, INC., TO SCHEDULE A 
MEETING BEFORE THE GOVERNING BODY REGARDING THE 
SANITATION REROUTING AND OPTIMIZATION PROJECT." 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne recognizes the need for residents 
to have a safe and convenient location to place their refuse cans for pickup by sanitation trucks; 
 
WHEREAS, particularly in older neighborhoods, alleys are a more convenient place for 
residents to store and place their refuse cans for pickup; 
 
WHEREAS, while sanitation services are a necessary component of everyday life, 
residents must receive these services from the City of Cheyenne pursuant to the Cheyenne 
municipal code, as opposed to a third-party service provider; 
 
WHEREAS, on May 13, 2024, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne approved a 
professional services agreement (PSA) between the City of Cheyenne and Univerus, Inc., (the 
Contractor) for the Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project, which agreement was in the 
amount of one hundred forty-two thousand six hundred seventy-six dollars ($142,676.00); 
 
WHEREAS, the PSA required that the Contractor complete its work on or before January 
31, 2025 and further required the company to present the findings from its analysis to the governing 
body; 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with the terms of the PSA, the Contractor analyzed the City of 
Cheyenne Sanitation Program for the purpose of identifying areas of optimization and efficiency, 
which analysis examined potential modifications to sanitation routes including changes to alley 
collection services for refuse pickup locations for Cheyenne residents; 
 
WHEREAS, as part of its work, the Contractor has concluded that efficiencies will be 
achieved by discontinuing current residential alley collection services in favor of collection at 
residential street curbs;  
 
WHEREAS, shifting collection from alley pickup to street curb pickup is a material change 
for sanitation customers and one that warrants public input, especially because sanitation services 
for residents under the municipal code must be provided through the City of Cheyenne; and 
 
WHEREAS, to date, the Contractor has not presented its analysis to the Governing Body 
as required under the terms of the PSA, nor has the City solicited any public comment on the 
1:09 pm, Nov 20, 2024

discontinuation of residential alley collection services in favor of collection at residential street 
curbs. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that in the interest of fostering community input and involvement, 
the Public Works Department is hereby requested to schedule a public meeting at a time and venue 
of public convenience, such as the Kiwanis Community House located in Lion's Park, to give 
Cheyenne residents the opportunity to provide public comment on this sanitation services issue; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Public Works Department is also strongly 
encouraged to solicit written public comment prior to the date of the public meeting to further 
allow residents to provide input on this issue, which solicitation could be accomplished through 
the use of the contact information provided under the Public Works Department portal on the City 
of Cheyenne website; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Public Works Department is expected to 
coordinate with the Contractor to schedule the required presentation before the Governing Body, 
through which the Contractor may present the results of its Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization 
Project as required by the PSA; and 
 
BE IT FINALLY RESOLVED, that the Public Works Department should endeavor to 
schedule the public meeting and required presentation on or before January 17, 2025. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_18904318.pdf
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Governing Body, City of Cheyenne 
FROM: 
 
Renee Smith, Economic Resource Administrator 
DATE: 
 
NOVEMBER 6, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
GRANT AGREEMENT BETWEEN THE CITY AND WYOMING OFFICE OF 
HOMELAND SECURITY TO SUPPORT EXPENSES AND EQUIPMETN FOR 
THE CHEYENNE FIRE RESCUE RERT#7 TEAM 
 
The City’s Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department has been awarded a Wyoming Office of Homeland Security 
(WOHS) grant through the US Department of Homeland Security Federal Emergency Management Agency, 
Grant Programs Directorate, State Homeland Security Program Grant FY2024.  
The Regional Emergency Response Team #7 is working to meet the qualifications of a Federal Emergency 
Management Agency Type II hazmat team which requires an increase in training requirements and equipment 
needs. RERT #7 requested funds for training to maintain current WMD/CBRNE and hazardous materials 
certifications. These funds will cover personnel costs, travel, instructors, and associated backfill. In addition 
to training, RERT #7 also included a request for grant funds to maintain existing equipment as well as purchase 
new equipment in order to fulfil these training requirements.  
WOHS received over $11 million in requests and only had $3.6 million in available funds. The grant award is 
$112,651 to support the expenses and equipment costs through a performance period of September 1, 2024 to 
August 31, 2026. There is no match requirement for this grant award. 
Please contact Renee Smith at rsmith@cheyennecity.org or Deputy Chief Andrew Dykshorn at 
adykshorn@cheyennecity.org if you have any questions. 
 
 

4:13 pm, Nov 05 2024

Article 1 
Assurances, Administrative Requirements, Cost Principles, Representations, and 
Certiﬁcations 
 
Recipients must complete either the Oﬃce of Management and Budget (OMB) Standard Form 424B Assurances – 
Non- Construction Programs, or OMB Standard Form 424D Assurances – Construction Programs, as applicable. 
Certain assurances in these documents may not be applicable to your program and the DHS ﬁnancial assistance 
oﬃce (DHS FAO) may require applicants to certify additional assurances. Applicants are required to ﬁll out the 
assurances as instructed by the federal awarding agency. 
 
Article 2 
General Acknowledgements and Assurances 
 
Recipients are required to follow the applicable provisions of the Uniform Administrative Requirements, Cost 
Principles, and Audit Requirements for Federal Awards in eﬀect as of the federal award date and located at 2 
C.F.R. Part 200 and adopted by DHS at 2 C.F.R. § 3002.10. All recipients and subrecipients must acknowledge 
and agree to provide DHS access to records, accounts, documents, information, facilities, and staﬀ pursuant to 2 
C.F.R. § 200.337. I. Recipients must cooperate with any DHS compliance reviews or compliance investigations. 
II. Recipients must give DHS access to examine and copy records, accounts, and other documents and sources of 
information related to the federal ﬁnancial assistance award and permit access to facilities and personnel. III. 
Recipients must submit timely, complete, and accurate reports to the appropriate DHS oﬃcials and maintain 
appropriate backup documentation to support the reports. IV. Recipients must comply with all other special 
reporting, data collection, and evaluation requirements required by law, federal regulation, Notice of Funding 
Opportunity, federal award speciﬁc terms and conditions, and/or federal awarding agency program guidance. V. 
Recipients must complete the DHS Civil Rights Evaluation Tool within thirty (30) days of receiving the Notice of 
Award for the ﬁrst award under which this term applies. Recipients of multiple federal awards from DHS should 
only submit one completed tool for their organization, not per federal award. After the initial submission, recipients are 
required to complete the tool once every two (2) years if they have an active federal award, not every time a federal 
award is made. Recipients must submit the completed tool, including supporting materials, to 
CivilRightsEvaluation@hq.dhs.gov. This tool clariﬁes the civil rights obligations and related reporting 
requirements contained in these DHS Standard Terms and Conditions. Subrecipients are not required to complete and 
submit this tool to DHS. The evaluation tool can be found at https://www.dhs.gov/publication/dhs- civil- rights-
evaluation-tool. DHS Civil Rights Evaluation Tool | Homeland Security. The DHS Oﬃce for Civil Rights and 

Attachment B to the Agreement between the Wyoming Office of Homeland Security 
and City of Cheyenne
